=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a string word and a non-negative integer k.
Return the total number of substrings of word that contain every vowel ('a', 'e', 'i', 'o', and 'u') at least once and exactly k consonants.
 
Example 1:

Input: word = "aeioqq", k = 1
Output: 0
Explanation:
There is no substring with every vowel.

Example 2:

Input: word = "aeiou", k = 0
Output: 1
Explanation:
The only substring with every vowel and zero consonants is word[0..4], which is "aeiou".

Example 3:

Input: word = "ieaouqqieaouqq", k = 1
Output: 3
Explanation:
The substrings with every vowel and one consonant are:

word[0..5], which is "ieaouq".
word[6..11], which is "qieaou".
word[7..12], which is "ieaouq".


 
Constraints:

5 <= word.length <= 250
word consists only of lowercase English letters.
0 <= k <= word.length - 5

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def countOfSubstrings(self, word: str, k: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's derive prefix method:
Let n = len(word). Let prefix consonants C[i] = number of consonants in word[0..i-1], i=0..n. For substring [l, r] (inclusive), consonants = C[r+1] - C[l]. Need = k => C[l] = C[r+1] - k. Let target = C[r+1] - k. Since C is nondecreasing by 0/1, indices l in [0, r] with C[l] == target form a contiguous interval [L_low, L_high] where:
- L_low = lower_bound(C, target) (first index with C >= target)
- L_high = upper_bound(C, target) - 1 (last index with C == target)
Need l <= r? Since C[l] <= C[r+1], target <= C[r+1], and if target > C[r]? Let's check. C[l] for l in 0..r. Since C[r] <= C[r+1]. If target = C[r+1]-k. But let's be absolutely certain prefix method counts exactly k consonants and all vowels. Let's prove with formal invariant.

For each right endpoint r, define:
- `pref[i]`: consonants in word[0:i].
- `last[v]`: last index <= r where vowel v appears.
- If any last[v] == -1, no valid substring ending at r.
- Else `m = min(last.values())`. For any l <= m, every vowel has last occurrence >= m >= l, so all vowels appear in word[l:r+1]. For any l > m, the vowel attaining m appears only before l (last occurrence < l), so not all vowels appear. Thus all-vowel condition equivalent to l <= m.
- Consonant condition: pref[r+1] - pref[l] = k => pref[l] = pref[r+1] - k = target.
- Since pref is nondecreasing, indices l with pref[l]=target form interval [lo, hi] found by binary search.
- Valid l count = size of intersection [lo, hi] ∩ [0, m] = max(0, min(hi, m)-lo+1) if lo <= min(hi,m). Since m <= r, l<=r automatically.

Need maybe if m = -1 skip. If target <0 skip. Good.

Need for prefix C:
```
pref = [0]*(n+1)
for i,ch in enumerate(word):
    pref[i+1] = pref[i] + (0 if ch in vowel_index else 1)
```
Then loop:
```
ans=0
last=[-1]*5
for r,ch in enumerate(word):
    if ch in vowel_index:
        last[vowel_index[ch]] = r
    min_last = min(last)
    if min_last == -1:
        continue
    target = pref[r+1] - k
    if target < 0:
        continue
    lo = bisect_left(pref, target)
    hi = bisect_right(pref, target) - 1
    if lo <= hi:
        high = min(hi, min_last)
        if lo <= high:
            ans += high - lo + 1
return ans
```

Now, let's decide final code. We can include:
```python
from bisect import bisect_left, bisect_right

class Solution:
    def countOfSubstrings(self, word: str, k: int) -> int:
        vowels = {'a','e','i','o','u'}
        vowel_id = {'a':0,'e':1,'i':2,'o':3,'u':4}
        n = len(word)

        pref = [0]*(n+1)
        for i,ch in enumerate(word):
            pref[i+1] = pref[i] + (0 if ch in vowels else 1)

        last = [-1]*5
        ans = 0

        for r,ch in enumerate(word):
            if ch in vowel_id:
                last[vowel_id[ch]] = r

            min_last = min(last)
            if min_last == -1:
                continue

            target = pref[r+1] - k
            if target < 0:
                continue

            lo = bisect_left(pref, target)
            hi = bisect_right(pref, target) - 1

            if lo <= hi:
                high = min(hi, min_last)
                if lo <= high:
                    ans += high - lo + 1

        return ans
```
Need note `ch in vowel_id` works for dictionary membership. We also use `ch in vowels` for prefix. Could use `vowel_id` for both:
```
if ch in vowel_id:
    pref[i+1] = pref[i]
else:
    pref[i+1] = pref[i]+1
```
Since vowel_id contains only vowels. Good. Use one dictionary.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "word = 'aeiouqqaeiou', k=0" and at r=5 target=1, C[l]=1 l=6, min0 no. Good.

Now,
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
class Solution:
    def countOfSubstrings(self, word: str, k: int) -> int:
        from bisect import bisect_left, bisect_right

        vowel_id = {'a': 0, 'e': 1, 'i': 2, 'o': 3, 'u': 4}
        n = len(word)

        # pref[i] = number of consonants in word[:i]
        pref = [0] * (n + 1)
        for i, ch in enumerate(word):
            pref[i + 1] = pref[i] + (0 if ch in vowel_id else 1)

        last = [-1] * 5
        ans = 0

        for r, ch in enumerate(word):
            if ch in vowel_id:
                last[vowel_id[ch]] = r

            min_last = min(last)
            if min_last == -1:
                continue

            target = pref[r + 1] - k
            if target < 0:
                continue

            lo = bisect_left(pref, target)
            hi = bisect_right(pref, target) - 1

            if lo <= hi:
                high = min(hi, min_last)
                if lo <= high:
                    ans += high - lo + 1

        return ans
```
